People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff — late meetings, small changes in how she messages me — but over the past year it’s lined up too neatly. It doesn’t feel accidental to me. It feels like there’s some private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if anyone else notices it. To me it feels like it’s meant for me, like the late meetings and the small changes in her messages are lined up too precisely to just be normal office stuff. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible I’m misunderstanding some of it. I know she says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. But after this long, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\n[Last Question]\nHas your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"It has mostly affected my marriage, with repeated tense conversations at home and the couples therapist eventually suggesting I get an individual assessment. I have not had major problems at work, with self-care, or keeping my usual routines.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. To me it feels like it’s meant for me, like the late meetings and the small changes in her messages are lined up too precisely to just be normal office stuff. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m here because I’ve been having trouble shaking this feeling about my wife’s work. She says her late meetings and small changes in her messages are just normal office things, but for about the past year they’ve lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually sending you these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff — late meetings, small changes in how she messages me — but over the past year it’s lined up too neatly. It doesn’t feel accidental to me. It feels like there’s some private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if anyone else notices it. To me it feels like it’s meant for me, like the late meetings and the small changes in her messages are lined up too precisely to just be normal office stuff. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible I’m misunderstanding some of it. I know she says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. But after this long, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly at home. It’s caused a lot of tense conversations with my wife, going over the same things again and again. We did see a couples therapist, and eventually they suggested I should get an individual assessment.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t think it’s really affected things much. I’m still keeping up with work, taking care of myself, and doing my usual routines. It’s mainly the marriage where it’s been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your work performance because of your thoughts about these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up for my teaching sections and keep up with the data analysis I’m supposed to do. I haven’t had anyone at work say there’s a problem.\n\nI’m still doing my usual things too, like riding my bike and meeting friends for chess. It’s more that it causes trouble at home, when my wife and I get stuck arguing about what her work schedule really means.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing in these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. So unless there was some clear reason for all of that, I think I’d still have a hard time believing it’s just coincidence.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often find yourself doubting the honesty of people besides your wife?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / teaching_assistant / graduate\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I’m here because I’ve been having trouble shaking this feeling about my wife’s work. She says her late meetings and small changes in her messages are just normal office things, but for about the past year they’ve lined up in a way that feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually sending you these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I know she says it’s just normal work stuff — late meetings, small changes in how she messages me — but over the past year it’s lined up too neatly. It doesn’t feel accidental to me. It feels like there’s some private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if anyone else notices it. To me it feels like it’s meant for me, like the late meetings and the small changes in her messages are lined up too precisely to just be normal office stuff. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible I’m misunderstanding some of it. I know she says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. But after this long, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly at home. It’s caused a lot of tense conversations with my wife, going over the same things again and again. We did see a couples therapist, and eventually they suggested I should get an individual assessment.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t think it’s really affected things much. I’m still keeping up with work, taking care of myself, and doing my usual routines. It’s mainly the marriage where it’s been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your work performance because of your thoughts about these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up for my teaching sections and keep up with the data analysis I’m supposed to do. I haven’t had anyone at work say there’s a problem.\n\nI’m still doing my usual things too, like riding my bike and meeting friends for chess. It’s more that it causes trouble at home, when my wife and I get stuck arguing about what her work schedule really means.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing in these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. So unless there was some clear reason for all of that, I think I’d still have a hard time believing it’s just coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting the honesty of people besides your wife?\n\nPatient: Not really. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible I’m misunderstanding some of it. I know she says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. But after this long, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly at home. It’s caused a lot of tense conversations with my wife, going over the same things again and again. We did see a couples therapist, and eventually they suggested I should get an individual assessment.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t think it’s really affected things much. I’m still keeping up with work, taking care of myself, and doing my usual routines. It’s mainly the marriage where it’s been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your work performance because of your thoughts about these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up for my teaching sections and keep up with the data analysis I’m supposed to do. I haven’t had anyone at work say there’s a problem.\n\nI’m still doing my usual things too, like riding my bike and meeting friends for chess. It’s more that it causes trouble at home, when my wife and I get stuck arguing about what her work schedule really means.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing in these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. So unless there was some clear reason for all of that, I think I’d still have a hard time believing it’s just coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting the honesty of people besides your wife?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I can get annoyed with people or wonder what they meant by something, but I don’t usually think other people are lying to me.\n\nThis is mostly about my wife.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have doubts about your wife's honesty when you're not thinking about her work schedule or messages?\n\nPatient: Mostly no. It really comes up around the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t sit around thinking she’s dishonest about everything. It’s just those things have felt too lined up over the past year, like there’s some private meaning there she isn’t saying directly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

It feels like there’s some private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without actually saying it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else notices these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know if anyone else notices it. To me it feels like it’s meant for me, like the late meetings and the small changes in her messages are lined up too precisely to just be normal office stuff. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her messages are just normal work things, but after a year of it, it feels too lined up to be random. It feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misunderstanding your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I mean… I suppose it’s possible I’m misunderstanding some of it. I know she says the late meetings and little changes in her work messages are just normal office things. But after this long, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s all accidental. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people would agree with your interpretation of your wife's actions?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure most people would agree, honestly. They’d probably say the late meetings and little changes in her messages are just normal work stuff, like she says. But to me, after a year of seeing it, it feels too precise to be accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly at home. It’s caused a lot of tense conversations with my wife, going over the same things again and again. We did see a couples therapist, and eventually they suggested I should get an individual assessment.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t think it’s really affected things much. I’m still keeping up with work, taking care of myself, and doing my usual routines. It’s mainly the marriage where it’s been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your work performance because of your thoughts about these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up for my teaching sections and keep up with the data analysis I’m supposed to do. I haven’t had anyone at work say there’s a problem.\n\nI’m still doing my usual things too, like riding my bike and meeting friends for chess. It’s more that it causes trouble at home, when my wife and I get stuck arguing about what her work schedule really means.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing in these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. So unless there was some clear reason for all of that, I think I’d still have a hard time believing it’s just coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting the honesty of people besides your wife?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I can get annoyed with people or wonder what they meant by something, but I don’t usually think other people are lying to me.\n\nThis is mostly about my wife.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have doubts about your wife's honesty when you're not thinking about her work schedule or messages?\n\nPatient: Mostly no. It really comes up around the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t sit around thinking she’s dishonest about everything. It’s just those things have felt too lined up over the past year, like there’s some private meaning there she isn’t saying directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals is based on reality?\n\nPatient: I do think it’s based on something real, yes. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and maybe that’s how it looks to other people.\n\nBut to me, over the past year, it’s lined up too precisely to feel accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the secret signals is something that could ever change, no matter what evidence or explanations you receive?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. But honestly, so far the explanations don’t really settle it for me.\n\nShe says it’s just normal work stuff, late meetings and little changes in messages, and I understand that’s what it sounds like. But it’s been going on for about a year, and it lines up too neatly in my mind to feel like coincidence. So I guess it would be hard for me to just drop the idea unless something really clearly showed me I was wrong.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would change even if your wife explained everything to you and proved there was no hidden meaning?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would change if she really explained everything, but honestly… I don’t know if it would completely. She already says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and I hear that. But after a year, the way they line up feels too precise to me. It still feels like there’s some kind of private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your belief in these secret signals is something that you're absolutely certain about, or is it something that you could potentially change your mind about?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m absolutely certain, no. I can admit there’s a chance I’m reading too much into it. But it’s hard for me to let go of, because over the past year the late meetings and the small changes in her work messages have seemed to line up too neatly. To me it feels like there’s a private signal in there, like she’s telling me something without saying it outright.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself thinking about these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: Pretty often. Not every minute, but most days I find myself going back over it in my head — the late meetings, the small changes in her work messages. I know she says it’s just normal office stuff, but it keeps feeling too lined up to be accidental, like there’s some message in it meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your belief in these secret signals affected your daily routine or relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, mostly at home. It’s caused a lot of tense conversations with my wife, going over the same things again and again. We did see a couples therapist, and eventually they suggested I should get an individual assessment.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t think it’s really affected things much. I’m still keeping up with work, taking care of myself, and doing my usual routines. It’s mainly the marriage where it’s been a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your work performance because of your thoughts about these secret signals?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I still show up for my teaching sections and keep up with the data analysis I’m supposed to do. I haven’t had anyone at work say there’s a problem.\n\nI’m still doing my usual things too, like riding my bike and meeting friends for chess. It’s more that it causes trouble at home, when my wife and I get stuck arguing about what her work schedule really means.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever stop believing in these secret signals from your wife?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’d like to be able to just let it go, but it doesn’t feel like something I can simply decide to stop believing.\n\nMy wife says the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages are just normal office things, and I hear that. But for about the past year, they’ve lined up too precisely for me to feel like it’s accidental. It still feels like there’s some private signal in them, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could ever convince you that your interpretation of your wife's actions is incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure what would convince me, honestly. I can understand the explanation that it’s just normal work stuff—late meetings, small changes in messages, things like that.\n\nBut the problem is, over the past year it’s all lined up too neatly for me to just dismiss it. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something without saying it directly. So unless there was some clear reason for all of that, I think I’d still have a hard time believing it’s just coincidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself doubting the honesty of people besides your wife?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I can get annoyed with people or wonder what they meant by something, but I don’t usually think other people are lying to me.\n\nThis is mostly about my wife.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have doubts about your wife's honesty when you're not thinking about her work schedule or messages?\n\nPatient: Mostly no. It really comes up around the late meetings and the little changes in her work messages.\n\nOutside of that, I don’t sit around thinking she’s dishonest about everything. It’s just those things have felt too lined up over the past year, like there’s some private meaning there she isn’t saying directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals is based on reality?\n\nPatient: I do think it’s based on something real, yes. I know she says the late meetings and those small changes in her work messages are just normal office stuff, and maybe that’s how it looks to other people.\n\nBut to me, over the past year, it’s lined up too precisely to feel accidental. It feels like there’s a private signal in it, like she’s trying to tell me something she won’t say directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the secret signals is something that could ever change, no matter what evidence or explanations you receive?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t want to say nothing could ever change my mind. But it’s been going on for about a year, and it lines up too neatly in my mind to feel like coincidence. So I guess it would be hard for me to just drop the idea unless something really clearly showed me I was wrong.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief in these secret signals would be considered irrational by most people?\n\nPatient: I can see why most people might call it irrational, yes. I know from the outside it probably sounds like I’m reading too much into normal work things.\n\nBut to me, the late meetings and those little changes in her messages have lined up too precisely for about a year. She says it’s nothing, but I feel like there’s a private signal there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife is actually hiding something from you with these signals?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think she’s hiding something, or at least not saying it directly.
